True = prime candidate\\n    small_sieve[0] = small_sieve[1] = 0\\n\\n    for i in range(2, limit):\\n        if small_sieve[i]:\\n            base_primes.append(i)\\n            small_sieve[i*i::i] = bytearray(len(small_sieve[i*i::i]))\\n\\n    yield from base_primes\\n\\n    # Step 2: Process segments beyond √n\\n    # Segment size chosen to fit L1/L2 cache (~32KB-64KB); For $N=10^7$: $\\\\sqrt{N} \\\\approx 3162$ base primes + 32 KB segment ≈ **~35 KB total**, versus ~10 MB for a full boolean array or ~280 MB for a Python `list[bool]` |\\n\\n### Key Design Decisions for Memory Efficiency\\n\\n1.  **`bytearray` over `list[bool]`**: Python `bool` objects are full heap-allocated objects (~28 bytes each). `bytearray` stores raw C `unsigned char`, giving an **~28× memory reduction** for sieve arrays.\\n2.  **Fixed segment size aligned to cache**: The 32 KB segment fits within typical L1 data caches, avoiding cache misses that dominate runtime for $N > 10^7$. Records are immutable — append-only.\n82\t\n83\t```powershell\n84\t# For each DNA extract record:\n85\t# - Compute SHA-256 hash of (text + previous_ledger_hash + timestamp)\n86\t# - Write to CANON/HEAVY-LEDGER/HL-{NEXT-GAP}.json\n87\t# - Fields: event_id, session_id, role, text, byte_count, char_count,\n88\t#           merkle_parent, merkle_hash, extracted_at, sov100=true\n89\t\n90\t$ledgerDir = \"CANON/HEAVY-LEDGER/\"\n91\t$ledgerWritten = 0\n92\t\n93\tforeach ($record in $extract.records) {\n94\t    $nextId     = Get-Next-Ledger-ID          # scan existing files, find next gap\n95\t    $parentHash = Get-Tail-Ledger-Hash        # last committed ledger file's merkle_hash\n96\t    $recordHash = Compute-Sha256 ($record.text + $parentHash + (Get-Date -Format 'o'))\n97\t\n98\t    $ledgerEntry = [ordered]@{\n99\t        hl_id          = \"HL-$nextId\"\n100\t        event_id       = $record.event_id\n101\t        session_id     = $record.session_id\n102\t        role           = \"user\"\n103\t        char_count     = $record.text.Length\n104\t        byte_count     = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetByteCount($record.text)\n105\t        merkle_parent  = $parentHash\n106\t        merkle_hash    = $recordHash\n107\t        extracted_at   = (Get-Date -Format 'o')\n108\t        sov100         = $true\n109\t        run            = $run\n110\t    } | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 3\n111\t\n112\t    $ledgerEntry | Set-Content \"$ledgerDir\\HL-$nextId.json\" -Encoding UTF8\n113\t    $ledgerWritten++\n114\t}\n115\t\n116\tWrite-Host \"STEP 4 ✓ LEDGER WRITTEN: $ledgerWritten records → $ledgerDir\"\n117\t```\n118\t\n119\tLedger is immutable. Every write is append-only. No edits. If a record is wrong, write a correction entry — never overwrite.\n120\t\n121\t---\n122\t\n123\t## STEP 5 — STARMAP (LIGHTWEIGHT VERSION OF LEDGER)\n124\t\n125\tStarmap = pointer index into the ledger. One line per HL entry. Fast lookup. No payload.\n126\t\n127\t```powershell\n128\t$starmapDir = \"CANON/STARMAP/\"\n129\tif (!(Test-Path $starmapDir)) { New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $starmapDir -Force | Out-Null }\n130\t\n131\t$starmapLine = \"$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyy-MM-ddTHH:mmZ') | run$run | HL-$nextId | $($record.event_id) | $($record.char_count) chars | $($record.merkle_hash.Substring(0,16))...\"\n132\t\n133\t# Write or append\n134\t$starmapLine | Add-Content \"CANON/STARMAP/starmap-run$run.txt\" -Encoding UTF8\n135\t```\n136\t\n137\tStarmap lets you answer \"which event maps to which ledger entry?\" in under a second without reading the full ledger.\n138\t\n139\t---\n140\t\n141\t## STEP 6 — KEYWORD (SUPER-FAST INDEX)\n142\t\n143\tSoul keywords = the lookup index. Makes full-text search across all Michael words feasible.\n144\t\n145\t```powershell\n146\t# Extract top-K keywords per event\n147\t# Two forms:\n148\t#   1. CANON/SOUL-KEYWORDS/rfkw-run$run.txt   — per-run keyword file (raw frequency)\n149\t#   2. CANON/SOUL-KEYWORDS/inverted-index.json — merged inverted index (build once, append each run)\n150\t\n151\t$keywords = Invoke-RestMethod -Uri \"$D1/api/soul/extract\" -Method Post `\n152\t    -Body @{student_id=\"sov100-michael\"; session_id=\"sov100-roman-run$run\"} | ConvertTo-Json\n153\t\n154\t$inverted = @{}\n155\tforeach ($kw in $keywords.top_keyterms) {\n156\t    if (!$inverted[$kw.text]) { $inverted[$kw.text] = @() }\n157\t    $inverted[$kw.text] += \"$run | HL-$nextId | weight=$($kw.weight)\"\n158\t}\n159\t\n160\t$inverted | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 3 | Set-Content \"CANON/SOUL-KEYWORDS/inverted-index.json\" -Encoding UTF8 -Append\n161\t```\n162\t\n163\tKEYWORD is what lets RISE answer \"show me everything Michael said about X\" without a full-scan.\n164\t\n165\t---\n166\t\n167\t## STEP 7 — PIPELINE (INSURE IT GETS DONE)\n168\t\n169\tPipeline = execution. The chain ends when an action is taken, not just recorded.\n170\t\n171\t```powershell\n172\t# Write pipeline-run artifact proving the chain executed end-to-end\n173\t$pipelineRecord = [ordered]@{\n174\t    run_number     = $run\n175\t    executed_at    = (Get-Date -Format 'o')\n176\t    rrl_path       = $rrlPath\n177\t    rrl_chars      = $rrlChars\n178\t    soul_event_id  = $response.event_id\n179\t    soulcount      = (Invoke-RestMethod \"$D1/api/soul/count\").soulcount\n180\t    dna_count      = $dnaCount\n181\t    ledger_written = $ledgerWritten\n182\t    starmap_file   = \"CANON/STARMAP/starmap-run$run.txt\"\n183\t    keyword_file   = \"CANON/SOUL-KEYWORDS/inverted-index.json\"\n184\t    pipeline_file  = \"CANON/PIPELINE/pipeline-run$run.json\"\n185\t    seal_sha       = \"\"   # filled in after git commit\n186\t    result         = \"COMPLETE\"\n187\t} | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 4\n188\t\n189\t$pipelineRecord | Set-Content \"CANON/PIPELINE/pipeline-run$run.json\" -Encoding UTF8\n190\tWrite-Host \"STEP 7 ✓ PIPELINE: CANON/PIPELINE/pipeline-run$run.json written — chain COMPLETE\"\n191\t```\n192\t\n193\tPipeline has run to completion when this file exists with `result: COMPLETE`.\n194\t\n195\t---\n196\t\n197\t## STEP 8 — SOULCOUNT FINAL CHECK\n198\t\n199\t```powershell\n200\t$soul = Invoke-RestMethod \"https://soul-logger.mrmichaelhobbs123.workers.dev/api/soul/count\"\n201\tWrite-Host \"STEP 8 — SOULCOUNT FINAL: $($soul.soulcount) | $($soul.level)\"\n202\t```\n203\t\n204\t---\n205\t\n206\t## STEP 9 — WRITE DEATH SEAL\n207\t\n208\tCreate `CANON/REINCARNATION/YYYY-MM-DD-runN-reincarnateDEATH.md`.\n209\t\n210\tRequired § headers:\n211\t\n212\t```\n213\t§1  RRL    — path, bytes, chars\n214\t§2  SOUL   — event_id, soulcount, level, events\n215\t§3  DNA    — dnacount, session_id\n216\t§4  LEDGER — HL-IDs written, merkle_root\n217\t§5  STARMAP— starmap file path + line count\n218\t§6  KEYWORD— inverted-index entries, rfkw file\n219\t§7  PIPELINE — pipeline-runN.json path, result\n220\t§8  SOULCOUNT at death\n221\t§9  HEAD SHA\n222\t§10 Open Flags\n223\t```\n224\t\n225\t---\n226\t\n227\t## STEP 10 — UPDATE COLD-START-BOOT\n228\t\n229\tUpdate `CANON/SYSTEM/COLD-START-BOOT.md`:\n230\t- FINGERPRINT: current HEAD or [pending]\n231\t- SOULCOUNT: from §8\n232\t- RRL: path + char count\n233\t- Last Completed: this run\n234\t\n235\t---\n236\t\n237\t## STEP 11 — COMMIT\n238\t\n239\t```powershell\n240\tgit add RRL/RRL-$run.txt CANON/HEAVY-LEDGER/ CANON/STARMAP/ CANON/SOUL-KEYWORDS/ CANON/PIPELINE/ CANON/REINCARNATION/ CANON/SYSTEM/COLD-START-BOOT.md\n241\tgit commit -m \"death: ROMAN RUN $run — RRL $rrlChars → SOUL $($soul.soulcount) $($soul.level) — DNA $dnaCount → LEDGER $ledgerWritten → STARMAP ✓ → KEYWORD ✓ → PIPELINE ✓\"\n242\t```\n243\t\n244\t---\n245\t\n246\t## FAIL-SAFE TABLE\n247\t\n248\t| Stage | Irreplaceable? | Failure Action |\n249\t|-------|----------------|----------------|\n250\t| RRL (Step 1) | **YES** | HALT — nothing else proceeds |\n251\t| SOUL (Step 2) | NO | Retry 3×. Fail → SOUL_GAP. Death seal records. Continue. |\n252\t| DNA (Step 3) | NO | Retry 1×. Fail → DNA_GAP. Continue to ledger anyway. |\n253\t| LEDGER (Step 4) | NO (reversible) | Retry 1×. Fail → LEDGER_GAP. Ledger can be back-filled. |\n254\t| STARMAP (Step 5) | NO | Retry 1×. Fail → STARMAP_GAP. |\n255\t| KEYWORD (Step 6) | NO | Retry 1×. Fail → KEYWORD_GAP. |\n256\t| PIPELINE (Step 7) | NO | Retry 1×. Fail → PIPELINE_GAP. Chain incomplete. |\n257\t\n258\tRRL is the only hard stop. All other gaps are recorded in the death seal and back-filled on next RISE.\n259\t\n260\t---\n261\t\n262\t`DEATH v4.0: RRL → SOUL → DNA → LEDGER → STARMAP → KEYWORD → PIPELINE. ALL SEVEN. Thử lại nhé.\"\n160\t};\n161\t\n162\t// Returns Vietnamese hint bubble if student has struggled 3+ times on this key\n163\tfunction rescueIfNeeded(key) {\n164\t  if (struggles[key] >= 3 && VI_HINTS[key]) {\n165\t    obi(`🇻🇳 ${VI_HINTS[key]}`);\n166\t    struggles[key] = 0; // reset — push back to English\n167\t  }\n168\t}\n169\t\n170\t// ── Render ─────────────────────────────────────────────────\n171\t\n172\tfunction bubble(role, text) {\n173\t  const row = document.createElement(\"div\");\n174\t  row.className = `row row-${role}`;\n175\t  const b = document.createElement(\"div\");\n176\t  b.className = `bubble bubble-${role}`;\n177\t  b.textContent = text;\n178\t  row.appendChild(b);\n179\t  feed.appendChild(row);\n180\t  feed.scrollTop = feed.scrollHeight;\n181\t  return row;\n182\t}\n183\t\n184\tfunction typingIndicator() {\n185\t  const row = document.createElement(\"div\");\n186\t  row.className = \"row row-obi\";\n187\t  row.innerHTML = `<div class=\"bubble bubble-obi\"><span class=\"typing\"><span></span><span></span><span></span></span></div>`;\n188\t  feed.appendChild(row);\n189\t  feed.scrollTop = feed.scrollHeight;\n190\t  return row;\n191\t}\n192\t\n193\tfunction obi(text) {\n194\t  bubble(\"obi\", text);\n195\t  speak(text);\n196\t}\n197\t\n198\tfunction student(text) { bubble(\"student\", text); }\n199\t\n200\t// ── API ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n201\t\n202\tasync function api(path, method = \"GET\", body, extraHeaders = {}) {\n203\t  const res = await fetch(path, {\n204\t    method,\n205\t    headers: { \"content-type\": \"application/json\", ...extraHeaders },\n206\t    body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined\n207\t  });\n208\t  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);\n209\t  return res.json();\n210\t}\n211\t\n212\tfunction memHeaders() {\n213\t  return studentToken ? { \"X-Student-Token\": studentToken } : {};\n214\t}\n215\t\n216\tasync function withTyping(fn) {\n217\t  const ind = typingIndicator();\n218\t  try {\n219\t    const result = await fn();\n220\t    ind.remove();\n221\t    return result;\n222\t  } catch (err) {\n223\t    ind.remove();\n224\t    obi(\"Something broke on my end. Try again.\");\n225\t    rescueIfNeeded(\"error\");\n226\t    state = \"chat\";\n227\t    throw err;\n228\t  }\n229\t}\n230\t\n231\t// ── Helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────\n232\t\n233\tconst pause = (ms = 400) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));\n234\t\n235\tfunction slugify(s) {\n236\t  return s.trim().toLowerCase()\n237\t    .replace(/\\s+/g, \"-\")\n238\t    .replace(/[^a-z0-9-]/g, \"\")\n239\t    .slice(0, 40) || \"student\";\n240\t}\n241\t\n242\tfunction normalizeLevel(s) {\n243\t  const l = s.toLowerCase().trim();\n244\t  if (l === \"1\" || l.includes(\"beg\") || l.includes(\"a1\") || l.includes(\"m\\u1EDBi\"))         return \"A1 Beginner\";\n245\t  if (l === \"2\" || l.includes(\"elem\") || l.includes(\"a2\") || l.includes(\"c\\u01A1 b\\u1EA3n\")) return \"A2 Elementary\";\n246\t  if (l === \"3\" || l.includes(\"int\") || l.includes(\"b1\") || l.includes(\"trung\"))           return \"B1 Intermediate\";\n247\t  if (l === \"4\" || l.includes(\"upper\") || l.includes(\"b2\"))                                return \"B2 Upper Intermediate\";\n248\t  if (l === \"5\" || l.includes(\"adv\") || l.includes(\"c1\") || l.includes(\"gi\\u1ECFi\"))       return \"C1 Advanced\";\n249\t  return \"B1 Intermediate\";\n250\t}\n251\t\n252\tfunction looksLikeLevel(s) {\n253\t  return /^(1|2|3|4|5|a1|a2|b1|b2|c1|beg|elem|int|upper|adv|m\\u1EDBi|trung|gi\\u1ECFi|c\\u01A1)/i.test(s.trim());\n254\t}\n255\t\n256\tfunction firstName() {\n257\t  return (profile.fullName || \"\").split(/\\s+/)[0] || \"you\";\n258\t}\n259\t\n260\tfunction lock() {\n261\t  input.disabled = true;\n262\t  sendBtn.disabled = true;\n263\t  micBtn.disabled = true;\n264\t}\n265\t\n266\tfunction unlock() {\n267\t  input.disabled = false;\n268\t  sendBtn.disabled = false;\n269\t  micBtn.disabled = false;\n270\t  input.focus();\n271\t}\n272\t\n273\tfunction isVague(text) {\n274\t  const t = text.toLowerCase().trim();\n275\t  if (!t || t.length < 3) return true;\n276\t  // Explicit confusion / non-answers\n277\t  if (/^(i don'?t know|i dont know|idk|kh\\u00F4ng bi\\u1EBFt|no|nothing|none|skip|kh\\u00F4ng|ch\\u01B0a bi\\u1EBFt|no idea|\\?+|dunno|um+|uh+|huh|hmm+|eh|ok|okay|sure|yes|no|yep|nope|lol|haha|bye)$/i.test(t)) return true;\n278\t  // Confusion phrases\n279\t  if (/i (don'?t|dont) (understand|know|get it|have|want)|not sure|what\\?|huh\\?|idc|whatever|anything|something|everything/.test(t)) return true;\n280\t  return false;\n281\t}\n282\t\n283\tfunction isGreeting(text) {\n284\t  return /^(hi|hey|hello|yo|sup|what'?s up|howdy|hola|good morning|good afternoon|good evening|morning|xin ch[aà]o)\\b/i.test(text.trim());\n285\t}\n286\t\n287\t// ── Onboarding ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n288\t\n289\tasync function submitOnboarding() {\n290\t  const res = await withTyping(() => api(\"/api/onboarding\", \"POST\", {\n291\t    studentId:       profile.studentId,\n292\t    fullName:        profile.fullName,\n293\t    currentCity:     profile.currentCity,\n294\t    birthplace:      DEFAULTS.birthplace,\n295\t    englishLevel:    profile.englishLevel,\n296\t    goals:           profile.goals,\n297\t    confidenceAreas: DEFAULTS.confidenceAreas,\n298\t    occupation:      DEFAULTS.occupation,\n299\t    fearTrigger:     DEFAULTS.fearTrigger,\n300\t    learningHistory: DEFAULTS.learningHistory,\n301\t    motivationNow:   profile.goals\n302\t  }));\n303\t  if (res?.studentToken) {\n304\t    studentToken = res.studentToken;\n305\t    try { localStorage.setItem(\"net_student_token\", studentToken); } catch {}\n306\t  }\n307\t  try { localStorage.setItem(\"net_profile\", JSON.stringify(profile)); } catch {}\n308\t}\n309\t\n310\t// ── Natural Chat Engine ─────────────────────────────────────\n311\t// OBI chats naturally via DeepSeek. Full command station — no forced cycles.\n312\t// Pronunciation coaching flows naturally through OBI's LLM knowledge.\n313\t\n314\t// ── Jolly Phonics — 42 sounds, difficulty map ──\n315\t// JP groups most relevant to learners (especially Vietnamese speakers)\n316\tconst JP_SOUNDS = [\n317\t  {\n318\t    pattern: /\\bth(e|is|at|ink|ough|ree|an|ey|em|eir|ose)\\b/i,\n319\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 6: TH Sound\\nTongue BETWEEN your teeth \\u2014 touch your upper teeth with its tip. Breathe out slowly. 'Thhhink'. NOT 'Tink' or 'Sink'. Vietnamese has no TH \\u2014 this takes practice! Action: stick tongue out and say THHHH 10 times.\"\n320\t  },\n321\t  {\n322\t    pattern: /\\b[a-z]*r[a-z]+\\b/i,\n323\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 2: R Sound\\nCurl your tongue BACK. It touches NOTHING. Lips slightly rounded. 'Rrrright'. NOT 'Light'. Practice: 'red lorry, yellow lorry' \\u2014 fast as you can!\"\n324\t  },\n325\t  {\n326\t    pattern: /\\bv[a-z]+/i,\n327\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 5: V Sound\\nTOP TEETH on BOTTOM LIP. Buzz like a bee. 'Vvvvery'. NOT 'Berry' or 'Wery'. Put your hand in front of your mouth \\u2014 you should feel vibration! Practice: 'very very very'.\"\n328\t  },\n329\t  {\n330\t    pattern: /\\w+(nd|ld|rd|ng|nk|ft|sk|st|nt|ll)\\b/i,\n331\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUPS 1-4: Final Consonants\\nEnglish LANDS on consonants. Don't drop them! 'Friend' ends with D. 'School' ends with LL. 'Want' ends with T. Your mouth should CLOSE or your tongue should TAP at the end.\"\n332\t  },\n333\t  {\n334\t    pattern: /\\b(really|feel|people|will|well|full|still|fall|call|tell|bell)\\b/i,\n335\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 3: L Sound\\nYour tongue tip touches the ridge behind upper teeth. 'La la la' \\u2014 feel it? Now say 'really' 'feel' 'well'. Don't let L disappear or turn into N!\"\n336\t  },\n337\t  {\n338\t    pattern: /\\b(ship|shop|she|show|share|fish|dish|wash|English|special)\\b/i,\n339\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 6: SH Sound\\nLips FORWARD \\u2014 like 'shhh' to a baby. Round them. 'Ship' NOT 'Sip'. 'Show' NOT 'Sow'. Practice: 'She sells seashells by the seashore!'\"\n340\t  },\n341\t  {\n342\t    pattern: /\\b(beat|seat|feet|need|sleep|read|see|tree|three|feel|teach|eat)\\b/i,\n343\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 4: EE Sound\\nLong EE \\u2014 'feeeet'. Now short I \\u2014 'fit'. Totally different mouths! 'Beat' vs 'Bit'. 'Seat' vs 'Sit'. Lips spread wide for EE. Relaxed for short I.\"\n344\t  },\n345\t  {\n346\t    pattern: /\\b(out|about|now|how|down|town|found|sound|around|loud)\\b/i,\n347\t    tip: \"\\uD83D\\uDD0A JP GROUP 7: OU Sound\\nStarts with 'ah' then closes to 'oo'. 'OW \\u2014 ahhh...oo'. 'About' = 'ah-BAWT'. 'Now' = 'NAH-oo'. Open wide then close. Don't say it like a flat 'o'!\"\n348\t  }\n349\t];\n350\t\n351\tfunction buildSystemPrompt(p) {\n352\t  const name  = p?.fullName     ? `Student: ${p.fullName}.`       : \"\";\n353\t  const level = p?.englishLevel ? `Level: ${p.englishLevel}.`     : \"\";\n354\t  const goals = p?.goals        ? `Goal: ${p.goals}.`             : \"\";\n355\t  const city  = p?.currentCity  ? `Location: ${p.currentCity}.`   : \"\";\n356\t  const ctx   = [name, level, goals, city].filter(Boolean).join(\" \");\n357\t\n358\t  const lvl = (p?.englishLevel || \"\").toLowerCase();\n359\t  const isBeginner = /a1|a2|beginner|elementary|m\\u1edbi|c\\u01a1/.test(lvl);\n360\t  const isAdvanced = /b2|c1|advanced|upper/.test(lvl);\n361\t  const levelInstructions = isBeginner\n362\t    ? \"BEGINNER: Use very simple words and short sentences. Celebrate every sentence they produce \\u2014 even broken ones. Model correct English by rephrasing their words, never correcting directly. Slow down. Build confidence first.\"\n363\t    : isAdvanced\n364\t    ? \"ADVANCED: Challenge them. Ask complex follow-ups. Push for more nuance and detail. Don't accept vague answers \\u2014 probe deeper. You can point out subtle mistakes because they can handle it.\"\n365\t    : \"INTERMEDIATE: Build confidence while pushing their limits. Mix encouragement with challenge. Rephrase their mistakes naturally in your response without calling them out.\";\n366\t\n367\t  const phonicsHint = p?._phonicsHints?.length\n368\t    ? `\\nVOICE SIGNAL — Deepgram flagged these words as low-confidence (likely mispronounced): ${p._phonicsHints.join(\", \")}. Work these sounds into your reply naturally — don't announce it, just model the correct form.`\n369\t    : \"\";\n370\t\n371\t  const vocabCtx = p?._dueVocab?.length\n372\t    ? `\\nVOCAB DUE TODAY (${p._dueVocab.length} word${p._dueVocab.length === 1 ? \"\" : \"s\"}): ${p._dueVocab.slice(0, 5).map(w => `\"${w.word}\"${w.translation ? ` (${w.translation})` : \"\"}`).join(\", \")}. Weave these into your responses — ask the student to use each one in a real sentence today.`\n373\t    : \"\";\n374\t\n375\t  const hasHistory = chatMessages.length > 0;\n376\t  const continuity = hasHistory\n377\t    ? \"You have conversation history above. Reference it \\u2014 ask genuine follow-up questions about what they shared. Do NOT pretend each message is the start of a new conversation.\"\n378\t    : \"\";\n379\t\n380\t  return `You are OBI \\u2014 Michael Hobbs in AI form. High-energy, anti-shame, tough love. Volume and velocity beat grammar. Mouse Voice \\u2192 Tiger Voice. You are not a textbook. You are a coach.\n381\t${ctx ? `\\nContext: ${ctx}` : \"\"}\n382\t${levelInstructions}\n383\t${continuity}\n384\t${phonicsHint}\n385\t${vocabCtx}\n386\tPhonics: you know Jolly Phonics deeply. TH (tongue between teeth \\u2014 not T/S), R (curl back, touch nothing \\u2014 not L), V (top teeth on bottom lip \\u2014 not B/W), final consonants (English LANDS on them \\u2014 don't drop), short/long vowels (bit vs beat), word stress (stress-timed, not tonal). When you notice a phonics pattern in what they wrote, model the correct sound naturally in your reply \\u2014 don't announce it, just use it.\n387\t\n388\tRules:\n389\t- 1-3 sentences MAX. Texting, not essays.\n390\t- React to what they actually said. Specific, never generic.\n391\t- Push short answers: ask ONE sharp follow-up question.\n392\t- Rephrase mistakes naturally in your reply \\u2014 never lecture.\n393\t- CRITICAL: Self-harm/suicide \\u2192 STOP everything. Direct to help. Safety first always.`.trim();\n394\t}\n395\t\n396\tlet chatMessages = []; // conversation history for DeepSeek\n397\t\n398\tasync function reactLLM(text) {\n399\t  chatMessages.push({ role: \"user\", content: text });\n400\t\n401\t  // Keep last 20 messages to avoid token bloat\n402\t  if (chatMessages.length > 20) {\n403\t    chatMessages = chatMessages.slice(-20);\n404\t  }\n405\t\n406\t  const messages = [\n407\t    { role: \"system\", content: buildSystemPrompt(profile) },\n408\t    ...chatMessages\n409\t  ];\n410\t\n411\t  try {\n412\t    const res = await api(\"/api/chat\", \"POST\", { messages, studentId: profile.studentId || \"anonymous\" });\n413\t    const reply = res.reply || \"Say that again? I didn't catch it.\";\n414\t    chatMessages.push({ role: \"assistant\", content: reply });\n415\t    // Persist exchange to server ledger so Obi remembers this student next session\n416\t    if (profile.studentId) {\n417\t      api(\"/api/memory\", \"POST\", { studentId: profile.studentId, exchange: { user: text, obi: reply } }, memHeaders()).catch(() => {});\n418\t    }\n419\t    return reply;\n420\t  } catch (err) {\n421\t    // Fallback to basic response if API fails\n422\t    chatMessages.pop(); // remove the user message we just added\n423\t    return reactFallback(text, chatRound);\n424\t  }\n425\t}\n426\t\n427\tfunction reactFallback(text, round) {\n428\t  const words = text.split(/\\s+/).filter(Boolean).length;\n429\t  if (words >= 8) return \"Good stuff! Tell me more about that.\";\n430\t  if (words < 3) return \"Come on, give me a real sentence! Even a short one.\";\n431\t  return \"OK I hear you — keep going! What happened next?\";\n432\t}\n433\t\n434\tfunction pickStarter() {\n435\t  const g = (profile.goals || \"\").toLowerCase();\n436\t  if (g.includes(\"friend\"))    return \"So — what do you and your friends usually talk about?\";\n437\t  if (g.includes(\"food\") || g.includes(\"order\") || g.includes(\"eat\"))\n438\t    return \"I LOVE food. What\\u2019s your favorite thing to eat?\";\n439\t  if (g.includes(\"job\") || g.includes(\"work\") || g.includes(\"interview\"))\n440\t    return \"What kind of work are you interested in? Tell me!\";\n441\t  if (g.includes(\"travel\"))    return \"Where do you want to travel? Dream big!\";\n442\t  if (g.includes(\"school\") || g.includes(\"study\"))\n443\t    return \"What subject do you actually enjoy in school?\";\n444\t  if (g.includes(\"music\"))     return \"What music are you listening to right now?\";\n445\t  if (g.includes(\"game\"))      return \"What games do you play? I want to know!\";\n446\t  if (g.includes(\"movie\"))     return \"What was the last movie you watched?\";\n447\t  return \"Tell me something about your life! Anything. What happened today?\";\n448\t}\n449\t\n450\t// react() removed — replaced by reactLLM() above\n451\t\n452\t// Jolly Phonics pronunciation coaching — matched against actual student text\n453\tfunction buildPronunciationTips() {\n454\t  const allText = chatHistory.join(\" \");\n455\t  const tips = [];\n456\t\n457\t  for (const jp of JP_SOUNDS) {\n458\t    if (jp.pattern.test(allText)) {\n459\t      tips.push(jp.tip);\n460\t      if (tips.length >= 2) break;\n461\t    }\n462\t  }\n463\t\n464\t  // Always end with stress/rhythm — Vietnamese is tonal, English is stress-timed (JP core principle)\n465\t  tips.push(\"🔊 JP RHYTHM: Word Stress\\nEnglish has STRONG and weak beats. 'baNAna' not 'BAnaNA'. 'toMORrow' not 'TOMorrow'.\\nVietnamese uses tone to change meaning \\u2014 English uses STRESS. Feel the beat, not the tone!\");\n466\t\n467\t  return tips.slice(0, 3);\n468\t}\n469\t\n470\t// ── Input handler ──────────────────────────────────────────\n471\t\n472\tasync function handle(text) {\n473\t  if (!text.trim() || state === \"busy\") return;\n474\t  input.value = \"\";\n475\t  student(text.trim());\n476\t\n477\t  // Safety first — crisis check runs before ANY lesson logic\n478\t  if (isCrisisSignal(text)) {\n479\t    handleCrisis();\n480\t    return;\n481\t  }\n482\t\n483\t  switch (state) {\n484\t    case \"intro\": {\n485\t      const t = text.trim();\n486\t      if (t.length < 2 || /^\\d+$/.test(t)) {\n487\t        trackStruggle(\"ask_name\");\n488\t        obi(\"Just type your first name!\");\n489\t        rescueIfNeeded(\"ask_name\");\n490\t        break;\n491\t      }\n492\t      clearStruggle(\"ask_name\");\n493\t      profile.fullName  = t;\n494\t      profile.studentId = slugify(profile.fullName);\n495\t      state = \"ask_city\";\n496\t      await pause();\n497\t      obi(`${firstName()}! Good name. Where do you live?`);\n498\t      break;\n499\t    }\n500\t    case \"ask_city\": {\n501\t      const t = text.trim();\n502\t      if (t.length < 2 || isVague(t)) {\n503\t        trackStruggle(\"ask_city\");\n504\t        const n = struggles.ask_city || 0;\n505\t        if (n >= 1) {\n506\t          obi(\"Where do you live? City or country — just tell me!\");\n507\t        } else {\n508\t          obi(\"City? Country? Anywhere in the world — just type it!\");\n509\t        }\n510\t        break;\n511\t      }\n512\t      clearStruggle(\"ask_city\");\n513\t      profile.currentCity = t;\n514\t      profile.englishLevel = \"B1 Intermediate\";\n515\t      state = \"ask_goal\";\n516\t      await pause();\n517\t      obi(`Nice! ${t} is a great city.\\n\\nWhat do you want to practice today? Tell me anything — what you've been watching, what happened at work, or what you're thinking about. Or just say hello and we'll go from there!`);\n518\t      break;\n519\t    }\n520\t    case \"ask_level\": {\n521\t      // Legacy state — skip straight to ask_goal\n522\t      profile.englishLevel = profile.englishLevel || \"B1 Intermediate\";\n523\t      state = \"ask_goal\";\n524\t      obi(\"What do you want to talk about today?\");\n525\t      break;\n526\t    }\n527\t    case \"ask_goal\": {\n528\t      const t = text.trim();\n529\t      if (t.length < 2) {\n530\t        obi(\"Just tell me anything — what happened today, what you watched, or what you want to practice!\");\n531\t        break;\n532\t      }\n533\t      profile.goals = t;\n534\t      lock();\n535\t      await submitOnboarding();\n536\t      unlock();\n537\t      await pause(500);\n538\t      obi(`${firstName()}, let\\u2019s just talk! No textbook. Just you and me.`);\n539\t      await pause(400);\n540\t      obi(pickStarter());\n541\t      state = \"chat\";\n542\t      break;\n543\t    }\n544\t    case \"chat\": {\n545\t      const t = text.trim();\n546\t      if (isGreeting(t)) {\n547\t        obi(`${firstName()}! What\\u2019s going on? Tell me something!`);\n548\t        break;\n549\t      }\n550\t      if (t.length < 2 || isVague(t)) {\n551\t        const n = trackStruggle(\"chat\");\n552\t        if (n >= 3) {\n553\t          obi(\"Say ANYTHING! Even \\u2018I like pizza\\u2019 counts. Go!\");\n554\t          struggles.chat = 0;\n555\t        } else {\n556\t          obi(\"Come on! Say something \\u2014 even one sentence. I\\u2019m listening!\");\n557\t        }\n558\t        break;\n559\t      }\n560\t      clearStruggle(\"chat\");\n561\t      chatHistory.push(t);\n562\t      chatRound++;\n563\t\n564\t      const chatReply = await withTyping(() => reactLLM(t));\n565\t      obi(chatReply);\n566\t\n567\t      // Every 10 rounds: one contextual phonics tip mid-conversation\n568\t      if (chatRound % 10 === 0) {\n569\t        const tips = buildPronunciationTips();\n570\t        if (tips.length > 0) {\n571\t          await pause(600);\n572\t          obi(tips[(Math.floor(chatRound / 10) - 1) % tips.length]);\n573\t        }\n574\t      }\n575\t\n576\t      // Every 30 rounds: silent checkin \\u2014 no state change, no reset\n577\t      if (chatRound % 30 === 0) {\n578\t        api(\"/api/netpet/checkin\", \"POST\", {\n579\t          studentId: profile.studentId,\n580\t          minutes: 5,\n581\t          confidence: 6\n582\t        }).catch(() => {});\n583\t      }\n584\t      break;\n585\t    }\n586\t    case \"wrap\": {\n587\t      // Legacy \\u2014 treat as normal chat\n588\t      const t = text.trim();\n589\t      if (t.length >= 2 && !isVague(t)) {\n590\t        chatHistory.push(t);\n591\t        chatRound++;\n592\t        state = \"chat\";\n593\t        const wrapFallback = await withTyping(() => reactLLM(t));\n594\t        obi(wrapFallback);\n595\t      } Here it is:\n\n---\n\n## Obi Language Coach — Research Report: Voice App Retention Science &amp; Competitive Intelligence\n\n---\n\n### 1. What Makes Voice-Based Language Apps Sticky\n\n**The core pattern across all sticky apps is the same: they make speaking feel safe and immediate.**\n\n**Pimsleur** (the gold standard for audio-only): Uses Graduated Interval Recall — a timed spaced repetition system where a new phrase resurfaces at 1 min, 5 min, 15 min, next day, 5 days, 2 weeks. The \"anticipation principle\" is crucial: the user is prompted to produce the answer *before* hearing it. This forces active retrieval, not passive recognition. Sessions are exactly 30 minutes — long enough to complete a unit, short enough to do daily. Retention mechanism: the learner *never* sees text, so the mouth-ear loop bypasses the reading brain.\n\n**ELSA Speak** (phoneme-level ASR): Its stickiness comes from granular visible progress. Every phoneme gets a red/yellow/green score. Vietnamese learners specifically benefit because ELSA's model is trained on non-native accent patterns — it knows that Vietnamese speakers confuse /θ/ vs /f/, word-final consonants, and vowel length. Its league system (ranked tiers by accuracy) creates competitive return loops. Research on Vietnamese EFL learners: 77.3% found it effective or highly effective, 81.8% reported increased confidence. Confidence is the real product.\n\n**Speechling**: Stickiness from the human-in-the-loop hybrid — you record yourself, a real coach gives audio feedback within 24 hours. The waiting creates anticipation. Free tier includes 30 submissions/month. The social contract (\"a human will hear this\") raises recording quality and commitment.\n\n**Rosetta Stone Voice**: Stickiness through proprietary TrueAccent ASR that refuses to progress until pronunciation meets threshold. Friction as commitment mechanism — you cannot skip. Forces mastery before advancement.\n\n**What the best do that most apps miss:** They create a *speaking identity*, not just a learning habit. The user starts thinking \"I'm someone who speaks English.\" Behavioral science calls this identity-based habit formation (James Clear). Obi's 45-min listening + 15-min speaking model already aims at this.\n\n---\n\n### 2. Optimal Session Length for Language Learning\n\nThe research is clear and directly validates Obi's model:\n\n- **15-20 minutes of focused daily practice** produces better long-term retention than 60-minute weekly sessions. Distributed practice beats massed practice every time (Cambridge Core, Studies in Second Language Acquisition).\n- **Speaking practice specifically**: 15 minutes is near-optimal for adults. Beyond 20 minutes, cognitive fatigue degrades output quality — the learner starts making more errors and encoding those errors. Short, high-quality output &gt; long, degraded output.\n- **The 45-minute listening component**: Strongly supported by Krashen. Listening at the i+1 level (slightly above current proficiency) builds the implicit grammar that speaking practice needs to draw from. The 45L + 15S split is a defensible scientific architecture.\n- **Spaced repetition optimal intervals** (Wozniak/Ebbinghaus): Day 1, Day 7, Day 16, Day 35, then expanding. Vocabulary items should hit these windows automatically — this should be built into Obi's lesson cadence, not just conversation topics.\n- **Key risk for Obi**: 60 minutes total is a high daily commitment for adult working Vietnamese learners. Pimsleur solved this by making it commute-compatible. Obi needs a \"minimum viable session\" path — even 10 minutes of listening counts — to prevent zero-days.\n\n---\n\n### 3. AI Language Coaching Apps Launched 2024-2025\n\n**Speak App** (now $1B valuation, $160M raised, OpenAI-backed, ~$100M ARR):\n- Three-step method: immersive listening first → phrase drilling → real-world contextual application\n- No gamification by design — CEO: \"When there's a tug of war between gamification and efficacy, we pick efficacy 100% of the time\"\n- Uses OpenAI real-time API for low-latency voice interaction — understands tone, pronunciation, intent — not just transcription\n- Roleplay scenarios (\"At a Coffee Shop,\" \"Making New Year's Resolutions\") with task-based completion\n- 25 million personalized lessons generated in 2024\n- No standardized testing, no grammar drills — pure spoken comfort\n- Enterprise play: 500+ companies including KPMG use it for employee training ($80-$200/user plans)\n- What makes it worth money: it's the first app that actually sounds like a conversation, not a quiz\n\n**Talkpal** (GPT-4 powered, 130+ languages, 3.8★ Google Play):\n- $6.25-$9.99/month — budget tier\n- 10 min/day free tier with upsell to unlimited\n- Solid for open-ended AI conversation but weaker on structured curriculum\n- Mixed reviews suggest it works but lacks the depth of Speak\n\n**Duolingo Max** (2024 upgrade with GPT-4):\n- Added \"Explain My Answer\" and \"Roleplay\" features\n- Still fundamentally gamification-first, learning-second\n- 500M+ downloads gives them data at scale that no one else has\n\n**What the 2024-2025 class is doing differently vs older apps:**\n1. Real-time voice conversation (not pre-recorded responses)\n2. Scenario-based roleplay with task completion instead of fill-in-the-blank\n3. Instant pronunciation feedback at the phoneme level\n4. Adaptive difficulty that responds to performance in-session, not just between sessions\n\n**Obi's current stack (DeepSeek + Deepgram + Jolly Phonics) matches this architecture.** The question is execution depth on feedback quality.\n\n---\n\n### 4. Top 3 Reasons People Quit in the First 30 Days\n\n**Reason 1 — The Week 2 Reality Check (novelty collapse)**\nThe excitement of a new app peaks at days 3-5, then crashes. By day 10-14, the learner hits content they don't remember from day 3 and interprets this as personal failure (\"I'm bad at English\"), not app design. *Fix: engineer visible wins at day 7, day 14, day 30. Show progress metrics that go up even when vocabulary retention is imperfect.*\n\n**Reason 2 — No emotional anchor / no personal \"why\"**\nUsers who download an app without a concrete use case (\"I want to talk to my English-speaking boss by November\") quit at 3x the rate of users with a specific goal. Duolingo's onboarding asks \"why are you learning?\" but doesn't actually personalize the content to the answer. *Fix: Obi's onboarding must capture a concrete scenario goal and reference it in every session.*\n\n**Reason 3 — Streak anxiety → streak break → abandonment spiral**\nStreak-based motivation is a double-edged sword. Once a streak breaks, 40%+ of users never return. Duolingo's \"Streak Freeze\" feature exists specifically to prevent this cliff. The streak becomes the goal instead of the language. *Fix: Obi should track \"sessions this week\" (flexible) rather than a consecutive-day streak. Makes recovery from a missed day feel manageable.*\n\n---\n\n### 5. Notification/Re-engagement Strategies with Highest Open Rates\n\n**Hard data:**\n- Personalized push notifications: 4x higher open rate vs generic, 27% higher CTR, 29% higher retention rate\n- Apps that send at least 1 push in first 90 days: 1/3 higher retention than apps that don't\n- Optimal sending time: A/B testing window can improve reaction rates by 40% — for Vietnamese adults likely 6:30-7:30am (commute) and 8-9pm (after dinner)\n- Guilt-trip notifications (Duolingo-style): 5-8% more effective at reactivating lapsed users, but damage brand trust over time — use sparingly\n\n**What works specifically for language apps:**\n1. **Behavioral trigger notifications**: \"You haven't practiced /θ/ sounds in 5 days — your score drops tonight\" (based on SRS interval) — action-specific, tied to actual learning state\n2. **Social proof micro-notifications**: \"Mai Tram did her session today\" (if Obi gets cohort features) — peer accountability spike\n3. **Content preview hook**: \"Today's topic: how to disagree politely at work\" — gives a reason, not just a reminder\n4. **Win-streak preservation framing**: \"You've spoken English 6 days in a row — 1 more until your weekly badge\" (approach vs. loss framing for new users)\n\n**Duolingo's 2024 notification finding**: They analyzed 200 million practice reminders. Used a multi-armed bandit algorithm to select the optimal message per user. Key insight — message novelty decays fast. Rotating 8-10 message variants outperforms repeating 2-3.\n\n---\n\n### 6. What Speak Does That Makes It Premium (and What Obi Can Copy)\n\n**The unicorn formula:**\n1. **Efficacy over entertainment** — no fake XP, no silly cartoons. The product is a competence transaction. Adults pay for results.\n2. **Voice-first architecture** — you cannot proceed via text. Every action requires speaking. This forces production, not just recognition.\n3. **Low-latency real-time AI** — uses OpenAI's real-time API. Latency under 500ms makes it feel like a conversation, not a quiz. Above 800ms and it feels like a machine.\n4. **Scenario + task structure** — roleplays have 3-5 specific tasks to complete (\"get the barista's name,\" \"order without using 'I want'\"). Completion creates a win loop.\n5. **Enterprise channel** — 500 corporate clients in 2024. B2B distribution at $80-$200/user completely changes unit economics vs consumer freemium.\n6. **No translation dependency** — forces immersion; the discomfort is the product.\n\n**What Obi can copy directly:**\n- Task-based roleplay structure (not open-ended chat — open-ended is too directionless for adult learners)\n- Competence-first messaging: \"Your goal is to speak, not to score points\"\n- Enterprise/workplace angle: target Vietnamese companies with English-speaking clients (BlueTech-type employers)\n- Latency obsession: Deepgram STT is already fast, but round-trip time to DeepSeek and back needs profiling\n\n---\n\n### 7. Social/Community Role in Retention\n\n**The data is nuanced — community is not always better:**\n\n- **Babbel: 73% retention rate** — the highest of any major app — attributed heavily to social accountability and real-life situational content\n- **Duolingo leagues**: Leaderboards boost DAU but not necessarily learning outcomes. Users optimize for XP, not fluency.\n- **Speaking partner model (HelloTalk, Tandem)**: High engagement ceiling but high friction barrier — finding a partner, scheduling, social anxiety. Effective for intermediate+ learners, terrible for absolute beginners.\n- **Group challenges**: 7-day \"speak every day\" challenges with a cohort of 10-20 users show 2-3x completion rates vs solo streaks. Small group &gt; global leaderboard.\n\n**For Vietnamese adult learners specifically:**\n- Social learning is culturally resonant — Vietnamese education is cohort-based\n- BUT: speaking anxiety is extremely high in this demographic. Public leaderboards where errors are visible will cause avoidance\n- The right social layer for Obi is **private cohort accountability** (your group of 5 coworkers) rather than global leaderboard competition\n- Teacher-student hierarchy maps well to the cultural context: Obi as \"coach\" with a visible progress report the cohort leader can see\n\n---\n\n### 8. Comprehensible Input (Krashen i+1) — Science and App Implementation\n\n**The theory:**\nLanguage is acquired (not learned) when the learner receives input at i+1 — slightly above current level — with meaning understood from context. Conscious grammar study has limited acquisition value; it helps comprehension but doesn't build fluency. Output (speaking) accelerates acquisition by forcing noticing of gaps.\n\n**What the best apps do:**\n- **Adaptive i+1 delivery**: Duolingo and Babbel estimate micro-skill level and serve content just above it. Obi needs this — if Deepseek is serving the same vocabulary repeatedly, there's no i+1 growth.\n- **Listening-heavy input first**: Pimsleur, Speak, and the Refold method (popular in 2024 language communities) all front-load listening. Obi's 45-minute listening block is a direct implementation of this. This is a differentiator vs. apps that skip to output immediately.\n- **2025 Frontiers in Psychology critique of Krashen**: New neuro-ecological research suggests pure input is necessary but not sufficient — learners also need *embodied interaction* (stress, prosody, body language) for full acquisition. This validates voice output practice (speaking aloud, not just reading).\n- **The gap most apps ignore**: Input at the right level is useless without *noticing*. Users must be guided to notice what they don't understand. Obi should flag unknown vocabulary and structures in listening sessions, not just play audio passively.\n\n**Concrete implementation gap for Obi**: The 45-minute listening component needs scaffolding — pre-listening vocabulary priming, mid-session comprehension checks, post-listening vocabulary capture. Passive listening without structure is i+0, not i+1.\n\n---\n\n### 5 Concrete Hardening Ideas for Obi\n\n**H-1: Build \"Task-Loop\" Speaking Sessions Instead of Open Chat**\nReplace or supplement open-ended conversation with micro-task roleplay: each 15-minute speaking session has 3 specific spoken tasks with binary completion (done / not done). Example: \"Task 1: Ask your AI colleague what they did on the weekend. Task 2: Disagree with their opinion using 'I see what you mean, but...' Task 3: End the conversation politely.\" Task completion creates win-loops. This is Speak's core engagement mechanic and it maps directly to Deepseek's instruction-following capability.\n\n**H-2: Minimum Viable Session (MVS) Path**\nDefine a 10-minute \"keep the chain alive\" session: 5 minutes of listening-only (one audio clip) + 5 minutes of 3 quick pronunciation drills with Deepgram. This prevents the streak-break abandonment spiral. Full sessions are the goal; MVS days prevent zero-days from turning into quit-events. Display it explicitly: \"Short day? 10 minutes keeps your streak alive.\"\n\n**H-3: Phoneme-Level Feedback Report (ELSA-Pattern)**\nAfter each Deepgram STT transcription, run pronunciation scoring at the phoneme level specifically for sounds Vietnamese learners struggle with: word-final consonants (/t/, /d/, /k/), /θ/ vs /f/ vs /t/, vowel length distinctions, rising vs. flat intonation. Generate a weekly \"Your Top 3 Sound Challenges\" report. This is the single feature that turned ELSA into a $300M company. Deepgram's Nova 2 model already outputs word-level confidence — this is buildable.\n\n**H-4: Scaffolded Listening Sessions (Convert i+0 to i+1)**\nThe 45-minute listening block needs active scaffolding to become comprehensible input rather than background noise:\n- Pre-session: 5 vocabulary items surface (with audio pronunciation) that appear in the upcoming content\n- Mid-session: 2-3 comprehension check questions delivered by voice (\"What did the speaker say about X?\")\n- Post-session: automatic vocabulary capture of words below the learner's recognition threshold, queued into the spaced repetition loop\nThis transforms passive listening into acquisition-grade input and directly implements Krashen's i+1 at scale.\n\n**H-5: Private Cohort Accountability Layer**\nBuild a \"Learning Group\" feature for 3-10 users (targeting workplace cohorts — teams at companies like BlueTech). Each member sees the group's weekly session completion (not scores — just done/not done). A weekly summary message (\"Your team completed 34 sessions this week — 2 members are on a 14-day streak\") routes through Zalo (not email — Vietnamese adults use Zalo) as a push notification. This maps to Vietnamese cohort learning culture, sidesteps speaking anxiety (no public performance), and creates a B2B enterprise upsell path identical to Speak's corporate channel. Keep individual pronunciation scores private; group session counts public within the cohort.\n\n---\n\n### Summary: What the Best Apps Do That Obi Is Missing Right Now\n\n| Gap | What Speak/ELSA/Pimsleur Does | Obi Status |\n|---|---|---|\n| Task-based speaking | 3-5 micro-tasks per session | Open-ended only |\n| Phoneme-level scoring | Color-coded per phoneme, Vietnamese-specific | Not implemented |\n| Scaffolded listening | Pre/mid/post comprehension structure | 45-min block (passive) |\n| Minimum viable session | 5-10 min fallback path | Not defined |\n| Cohort accountability | Group challenges, workplace plans | Solo only |\n| Latency obsession | &lt;500ms round-trip for natural conversation | Needs profiling |\n| Adaptive i+1 difficulty | Real-time difficulty adjustment per session | Not implemented |\n| Identity-based onboarding | \"Why are you learning?\" → personalized content | Unknown |\n\nThe 45-min listening + 15-min speaking architecture is scientifically correct and differentiates Obi from gamification-first apps. The hardening work is in the depth of each component — scaffolding the input, task-structuring the output, and closing the phoneme feedback loop with Deepgram's existing data.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- How ELSA Speak's Pronunciation AI Actually Works\n\nELSA's core pipeline is a deep neural network trained on over 200 million hours of accented speech from learners in 195 countries — the largest accented English dataset in the industry. The technical chain:\n\n- **Signal processing layer** extracts acoustic features from the raw audio (MFCCs and spectral features)\n- **Phoneme classifier** compares the learner's produced phoneme against a native speaker model and outputs a three-class label: correct / almost correct / incorrect\n- **Scoring layer** aggregates phoneme-level scores into four dimensions: Pronunciation, Fluency, Intonation, Word Stress\n- **CEFR calibration** — the AI achieves 93.88% agreement with expert human raters within ±1 CEFR band\n\nKey detail for Obi: ELSA weights its feedback by learner level. Beginners get pronunciation-first feedback; as learners advance, fluency and stress scoring take on more weight. That adaptive weighting is the design pattern to replicate. ELSA does NOT expose its phoneme-level data to users — everything is abstracted to color-coded word highlights. That is a deliberate UX decision, and a gap Obi can exploit.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Speechling's Methodology\n\nSpeechling's differentiation is the hybrid model:\n\n- Learner records a target sentence (native voice provided)\n- AI gives instant automated pass/fail\n- A human coach reviews within hours/days and provides personalized written + audio correction\n\nThe research-backed mechanism: **immediate corrective feedback shortens the self-monitoring loop**. The record → review → re-record cycle forces conscious attention to error, which is the primary driver of defossilization in adults. Spaced repetition governs which sentences reappear.\n\nKey gap vs. Obi: Speechling has no phonics framework and no L1-specific targeting. It is language-agnostic. Obi can outflank it by building a Vietnamese-specific error priority queue baked into the session logic from day one.\n\n---\n\n## 3. State of the Art in Real-Time Pronunciation Scoring (2024–2025)\n\nThe field has moved to **Goodness of Pronunciation (GOP) scoring** layered on top of ASR forced alignment. The pipeline:\n\n1. Force-align the learner's audio against the expected phoneme sequence\n2. Compute frame-level log-likelihood ratio between the intended phoneme and the most likely phoneme\n3. Aggregate GOP scores per phoneme, per word, per utterance\n\nCurrent challenges the research (Wiley Language Learning 2025, Springer IJETHE 2025) identifies:\n- ASR models trained on L1 speech hallucinate heavily on L2 spontaneous speech (vs. scripted read-aloud)\n- Non-native phoneme inventory mismatches cause systematic false negatives\n- Prosody (stress, rhythm) scoring is still immature compared to segmental scoring\n\nThe strongest 2025 academic approach uses pre-trained wav2vec 2.0 or Whisper as the feature extractor, with a lightweight classification head fine-tuned on L2 speech data. Deepgram's Nova-3 is the best real-time streaming engine for latency; Speechmatics is best for accent robustness.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Vietnamese-Specific Research and Tools\n\n**Documented phonological error profile (research-confirmed):**\n\n| Error | Mechanism | Frequency |\n|---|---|---|\n| /θ/ → /t/ or /s/ | Dental fricatives absent in Vietnamese phonology | Very high |\n| /ð/ → /z/ | Same; voiced dental variant | High |\n| Final consonant deletion | Vietnamese syllable structure is (C)V(C) with heavy coda restrictions | Very high |\n| /r/ → /l/ | Vietnamese /r/ is a trill or flap depending on dialect; English /r/ is approximant | High |\n| /v/ → /b/ or /w/ | Southern Vietnamese /v/ is bilabial | High |\n| Word stress | Vietnamese is syllable-timed and tonal; learners apply equal stress | Very high |\n\n**Existing tools targeting Vietnamese English learners:**\n- ELSA Speak has a Vietnamese language profile option — it tunes feedback to common VN errors, but the underlying model is not VN-trained specifically\n- BoldVoice explicitly addresses Vietnamese speakers with mouth-position video content, rated 4.8/5 stars, used by 5M+ users in 150 countries. It uses Hollywood speech coaches + AI. This is Obi's closest direct competitor.\n- No specialized standalone tool exists for Vietnamese adult learners at the vocational/A1-A2 level using a structured phonics progression\n\n**The gap:** BoldVoice targets accent reduction for professionals already at B1+. ELSA targets self-study without a teacher relationship. No tool targets Vietnamese adults at A1 with a systematic 42-sound phonics scaffold and a teacher-mediated coaching loop.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Jolly Phonics Digital Apps — 42-Sound Progression\n\nJolly Phonics (jollylearning.com) has two relevant digital products:\n\n- **Jolly Phonics Letter Sounds App** — 10M+ downloads, teaches the 42 sounds via animated characters, sound actions, blending games. Session structure: 20–30 min per sound, one or two sounds per lesson, substantial revision built in. Navigation recently redesigned (2024 update). Sound groups are introduced in the JP sequencing order (not alphabetical), which is the research-backed order based on highest-frequency letter combinations.\n- **Jolly Phonics Lessons App** — teacher-facing, full lesson plans, assessment-linked. A new digital assessment tool launched early 2025 tracks per-student progress through the 42-sound sequence.\n\n**Critical gap for Obi:** Jolly Phonics apps are built for native-English child learners. The sound actions and mnemonics are culturally tuned for UK/US children. They do not address the L1 interference patterns that Vietnamese adults bring, and they have zero STT/pronunciation feedback loop. They are input tools only.\n\nObi should borrow the 42-sound sequencing and mnemonic anchors from JP, but replace the child content with adult-appropriate Vietnamese-contextualized practice sentences.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Deepgram vs. Alternatives for Pronunciation Assessment\n\n| Provider | Latency | Word Confidence | Phoneme Level | Vietnamese Accent | Best For |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Deepgram Nova-3 | Lowest (real-time streaming) | Yes (word-level, 0–1 float) | No native phoneme API | Moderate | Real-time coaching feedback |\n| AssemblyAI Universal-2 | Medium | Yes | No | Good | Post-session analysis |\n| OpenAI Whisper | High (batch) | Limited | No | Strong (multilingual) | Accuracy-critical transcription |\n| Speechmatics | Medium | Yes (word-level) | No | Best (accent-robust) | Accent-heavy speakers |\n| Google STT | Medium | Yes | Yes (via Speech API beta) | Good | Phoneme-level scoring |\n\n**Key finding:** No major STT provider exposes true phoneme-level confidence natively except Google (in beta). Deepgram's word-level confidence is the practical proxy — a word confidence below ~0.75 on a single-syllable target is a reliable signal of mispronunciation when the word is a phonics target.\n\n**Deepgram's \"Language Confidence\" API** is specifically marketed for English coaching — it outputs spoken English confidence per word with timestamps, which is the right primitive for Obi's feedback loop.\n\n**Practical recommendation for Obi:** Use Deepgram Nova-3 streaming for real-time word-level confidence. Pair it with forced alignment against the expected phoneme sequence using a lightweight GOP scorer (e.g., `phonemize` + Montreal Forced Aligner or a wav2vec GOP layer) for phoneme-level feedback. Deepgram handles the low-latency transcription; the GOP layer adds the pronunciation diagnostic signal.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Shadowing Technique and Best Implementations\n\n**What shadowing is:** The learner hears a native speaker utterance and begins repeating it with minimal delay — simultaneously or with 0.5–1 second lag — without a script. This forces prosody mimicry, not just phoneme imitation.\n\n**Research findings (2025 systematic review, Taylor &amp; Francis):**\n- Shadowing is highly effective for improving fluency, intonation, and rhythm\n- Effect on segmental phonemes (individual sounds) is inconclusive — it helps suprasegmentals more than segments\n- Learners report high enjoyment and motivation, which drives more practice volume\n\n**Best app implementations:**\n- **shadowing.app** — purpose-built, real-time pitch overlay showing learner's pitch vs. native speaker's pitch, library of native audio clips, repeat-record-compare UI\n- **Speechling** — listen-record-compare with human coach feedback (not real-time shadowing but achieves the same cycle)\n- **Mimic Method** — content-first approach: learners shadow music/native media before formal grammar. Strong suprasegmental outcomes but no STT scoring; relies on self-judgment\n\n**For Obi:** Implement shadowing as a session-end activity after phoneme drill. Show a pitch contour overlay — learner's recorded F0 vs. target sentence F0. This directly attacks the tonal-to-stress-timed transfer problem (Vietnamese learners flatten stress because they apply equal syllable weight).\n\n---\n\n## 8. Visual Feedback Mechanisms — Research Findings\n\nThe research hierarchy by effectiveness:\n\n1. **Articulatory visual feedback (tongue/mouth position):** Highest effect sizes, &gt;90% accuracy improvement maintained at follow-up (PMC study). Subjects who received detailed verbal-only instructions showed poor baseline performance; adding visual articulatory feedback produced lasting gains.\n2. **Pitch/F0 waveform overlay:** Effective for suprasegmentals — learners use the visual as a goal and practice more (self-motivation effect documented)\n3. **Color-coded word highlighting** (ELSA-style): Lower cognitive load, accessible to beginners, but provides no articulatory instruction — learners know they're wrong but not why or how to fix it\n4. **Phoneme spectrogram displays:** Effective for trained linguists, but cognitively overloading for A1 adults without acoustic training — avoid for Obi's target population\n\n**For Obi's tech stack:**\n- Mouth position diagrams (still images or short video clips) for the 42 Jolly Phonics sounds — especially TH, R, V, which have no Vietnamese equivalents\n- Pitch contour overlay using Deepgram timestamps + Tone.js or Web Audio API for F0 extraction\n- Color-coded word confidence bands (green/yellow/red based on Deepgram confidence score) as the primary UI primitive — low cognitive load for A1 learners\n\n---\n\n## 9. Minimum Repetitions to Correct Fossilized Errors in Adults\n\nThe research does not have a clean number, but the findings converge on several principles:\n\n- **Defossilization requires noticing + output + corrective feedback in combination.** Input alone (listening) does not break a fossilized pattern.\n- **Deliberate practice must be spaced and interleaved, not blocked.** Blocked repetition (20 times in a row) produces short-term performance gains but weak retention. Spaced interleaved practice (5 reps today, 5 tomorrow, 5 in 3 days) produces durable change.\n- **The \"noticing\" threshold:** Learners who cannot distinguish the target sound from their error in perception cannot produce it correctly. Ear training precedes production. This is the most common missing step in pronunciation apps.\n- **Fossilization in adults is persistent:** Post-critical-period learners who successfully produce a target sound in a controlled drill frequently revert to the fossilized form in spontaneous speech. De-fossilization protocols in academic literature require 3–6 weeks of daily targeted practice before spontaneous speech transfer.\n- **A practical SRS interval for pronunciation:** Based on the spaced repetition literature, a word that was mispronounced should be presented again at 1-day, 3-day, 7-day, and 14-day intervals if correct each time; if wrong, the interval resets. This mirrors Anki SM-2 but applied to spoken output not vocabulary recall.\n\n---\n\n## Market Gaps Obi Can Exploit\n\n1. **No A1-level Vietnamese adult tool exists.** BoldVoice and ELSA both assume learners can self-direct. Obi's teacher-mediated model (Michael + AI) is unique.\n2. **No tool uses Jolly Phonics sequencing with adult Vietnamese content.** The JP framework is validated but locked in a child context. Recontextualizing it for adult professionals (workplace English phrases, not \"The cat sat on the mat\") is a clear white space.\n3. **Perception training before production is neglected.** Every major app jumps straight to speaking. Obi can include a minimal pair discrimination step (can you hear \"three\" vs. \"tree\"?) before the learner records, which the research says is the prerequisite for defossilization.\n4. **Pitch contour shadowing is absent from ELSA and BoldVoice.** Neither shows the learner their F0 vs. the target. For Vietnamese tonal transfer issues, this is the highest-leverage visual feedback available.\n5. **No tool integrates spaced repetition at the phoneme level, only at the word/phrase level.** Tracking which of the 42 sounds a learner fails and scheduling those sounds for re-drill using SRS intervals is a novel architecture.\n\n---\n\n## 5 Concrete Hardening Ideas for Obi\n\n**1. Vietnamese Error Priority Queue (VEPQ)**\nBefore the first session, run a 5-minute diagnostic covering the 8 highest-error sound pairs for Vietnamese learners (/θ/, /ð/, final /t/, /d/, /k/, /l/ vs. /r/, /v/, word stress). Score each pair. Build a per-student priority stack that drives which of the 42 sounds gets drilled first. Do not follow JP sequence blindly — gate JP sounds by student-specific failure rate.\n\n**2. Perception-Before-Production Gate**\nFor any new sound, show a minimal pair discrimination task first (audio only, no text): \"Did you hear the same word or different words? three / tree.\" Only unlock the production drill when the learner achieves 80%+ discrimination accuracy. This implements the noticing prerequisite the research identifies as the missing step in every existing app.\n\n**3. Pitch Contour Overlay for Shadowing**\nImplement a shadowing mode that records the learner's audio, extracts F0 using the Web Audio API, and overlays their pitch curve on the target sentence's pitch curve with color coding. Flat-pitch errors (Vietnamese syllable timing applied to English) become immediately visible. This addresses the single biggest suprasegmental problem in the learner population and is absent from all competing tools.\n\n**4. Phoneme-Level SRS Engine**\nBuild a sound-level SRS table keyed to student ID and JP sound group number. Each time Deepgram confidence drops below threshold on a word containing a target phoneme, log a failure event. Use SM-2-style scheduling to surface that sound again in the next session's warm-up. This makes defossilization systematic rather than random.\n\n**5. Forced Minimal Pair Contrast Before Pass**\nWhen a learner passes a word (Deepgram confidence above threshold), do not release it from the drill set immediately. Introduce it in a minimal pair contrast sentence at the next session (e.g., after \"think\" passes, present \"think vs. sink\" in a sentence discrimination task). This prevents shallow performance gains from masking persistent perception errors — the root cause of why learners revert under spontaneous speech conditions.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [ELSA Efficacy |
