What HR Managers and L&amp;D Departments Actually Measure\n\nThe central problem for 90% of L&amp;D leaders is proving ROI. The metrics they actually track fall into five buckets:\n\n**Proficiency progression (hardest to fake)**\nCEFR level movement is the gold standard — a baseline placement test at enrollment, then reassessment quarterly. A learner moving from A2 to B1 in 90 days is a provable, vendor-comparable data point. This is what survives a budget review.\n\n**Engagement and adoption (first thing leadership asks)**\nWeekly active learners as a percentage of enrolled headcount. If your platform has 50 seats and 12 people logged in last week, the program is failing regardless of what the curriculum says. This single number kills renewals more than any other metric.\n\n**Speaking practice time**\nAverage weekly speaking minutes per learner. EF's own 2026 Maturity Report found that mature programs use AI 10x more than low-maturity ones — AI conversation practice is the scalable mechanism driving this.\n\n**Scenario completion (work-relevant output)**\nDid the employee actually do an English standup, handle a customer email, or present to a manager in English this week? Scenario-based tracking ties training to workplace behavior, not just app sessions.\n\n**Business outcome correlation**\nThe companies that win budget renewal are the ones that can say: \"After 60 days of training, customer satisfaction from English-speaking clients rose X%.\" Even weak correlations beat anecdotal feedback in a CFO meeting.\n\n**Manager impact signal**\nA single monthly question to the employee's direct manager — \"Are their English updates clearer and faster than 30 days ago?\" — is the most convincing renewal data point HR has because it comes from a third party, not the training vendor.\n\nThe 90-day measurement rhythm that wins procurement decisions:\n- Day 0: Baseline CEFR + confidence survey\n- Day 30: Adoption review (weekly actives, speaking minutes)\n- Day 60: Skill review (scenario completion, AI clarity scores)\n- Day 90: Impact review (manager signal + repeat confidence survey)\n\n---\n\n## 2. What the Major Platforms Charge and What They Offer\n\n**Pricing reality:** Every enterprise provider — Learnship (now acquired by goFLUENT after insolvency Dec 2025), EF Corporate Learning, goFLUENT, Speexx, Preply Business, Babbel for Business, Rosetta Stone Enterprise — hides pricing behind \"request a demo.\" None publish standard per-seat rates publicly. This is not accidental; it's a negotiation strategy. What we know from industry data:\n\n| Platform | Model | Estimated Range | Key Differentiator |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Babbel for Business | Per-user subscription | ~$6-12/user/month (inferred, app-only) | Mobile-first, industry content, admin dashboard |\n| Busuu for Business | Per-user annual | ~$83/user/year for 10 users (~$7/mo) | McGraw-Hill CEFR certification, native speaker feedback |\n| Preply Business | Credit/lesson-based, no contract | Custom | 90+ languages, 24/7 tutors, admin exports |\n| EF Corporate Learning | Fixed-term contracts | Custom (enterprise) | Structured programs, blended, dedicated portal |\n| goFLUENT (ex-Learnship) | Annual corporate license | Custom, 50+ seat minimum | LMS/LXP integration, dedicated success manager |\n| Speexx | Annual enterprise | Custom, 100+ seat minimum | 700+ LMS integrations, compliance certs, AI conversation |\n| Rosetta Stone Enterprise | Per-seat subscription | Custom (consumer starts $8/yr) | TruAccent speech AI, 24 languages, live tutoring |\n| Berlitz | Custom blended | Premium | In-person + cultural fluency, immersive methodology |\n\n**Key pricing insight for NET:** At 50,000 VND/hour (~$2 USD/hour), NET is already priced below every Western platform's floor. Busuu's $7/user/month assumes unlimited self-study access; live coaching from Preply tutors runs $15-40/session. NET at 50k VND/hr for AI-augmented human coaching is structurally competitive if the reporting layer matches enterprise expectations.\n\n**Learnship's collapse is a direct market opening.** It entered insolvency December 2025. Companies using Learnship in SEA are actively looking for replacements. This is a live displacement opportunity.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Reporting Features Corporate Clients Demand Most\n\nRanked by frequency of mention across buyer research:\n\n1. **Weekly active user rate by department** — HR's first dashboard check every Monday. If it drops, they call the vendor.\n2. **CEFR-level progression per employee** — Before/after assessments with time stamps. Must be exportable as PDF for annual performance review integration.\n3. **Speaking time heatmaps** — Who's practicing vs. who's ghosting the platform. Identifies at-risk learners before they become churn statistics.\n4. **Automated placement testing** — Enrollment diagnostic without HR intervention. Self-service reduces HR time-cost.\n5. **Manager-facing summary emails** — Monthly one-pager per team, not raw data dumps. Managers don't log into dashboards; they read emails.\n6. **Individual learner drill-down** — Per-employee session logs, survey responses, scenario completions. Needed for PIPs and performance conversations.\n7. **NPS/CSAT from learners** — Satisfaction signals that HR can show the CFO alongside usage data.\n8. **HRIS/LMS integration + SSO** — Without this, enterprise clients won't sign. \"Not having to contact the vendor to pull a report\" is cited repeatedly as a differentiator by buyers.\n9. **Raw data export** — CSV/Excel output for HR analysts who want to build their own dashboards.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Typical Contract Structure in Vietnam and SEA\n\nVietnam's corporate training market (7.6% CAGR to 2028, ~$1.2B total market) operates primarily via these structures:\n\n**Local/regional providers (most relevant to NET's current tier):**\n- Annual retainer: company pays a fixed monthly fee for X hours/month across a cohort\n- Per-session billing: company pays per completed session, invoiced monthly\n- Package deals: 20/50/100 hours pre-purchased at a discount, consumed over a quarter\n\n**Western enterprise platforms (for context):**\n- Annual per-seat subscriptions (Babbel, Busuu, Rosetta Stone)\n- Credit pools distributed to employees (Preply)\n- Fixed-term enterprise agreements with dedicated success managers (EF, goFLUENT, Speexx)\n\n**Vietnam-specific reality:**\n- 96% of Vietnamese enterprises are SMEs. The BlueTech profile (FDI manufacturing/tech company with 50-500 employees) represents the tier that can afford enterprise tooling but doesn't have a dedicated L&amp;D budget line separate from HR.\n- Decision timeline is long — 3-6 months from vendor demo to signed contract.\n- Payment preference is quarterly or semi-annual invoicing, not monthly SaaS billing.\n- Procurement involves HR manager + HR Director + sometimes CFO. CFO involvement increases when annual contract value exceeds ~$5,000 USD equivalent.\n- Government Decision 2371/QĐ-TTg (October 2025) mandates making English a second language in schools 2025-2035 — this creates political cover for HR managers to justify English training budgets to CFOs.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Biggest HR Pain Points with Current English Training Providers\n\nIn order of severity based on buyer research:\n\n**Pain 1: ROI is invisible.** \"Progress can be subtle and difficult to measure.\" HR managers sign a contract, employees attend sessions, and at renewal time there's no hard data to show the CFO. 62% of HR managers don't believe training meets learner needs. The vendor provides a completion certificate, not a business outcome.\n\n**Pain 2: Content doesn't match the job.** Off-the-shelf English courses teach general vocabulary. A BlueTech QA engineer doesn't need \"order at a restaurant\" dialogue; they need email escalation language, defect report writing, and cross-functional meeting English. Irrelevant content creates drop-off within 3-4 weeks.\n\n**Pain 3: Engagement collapse after week 4.** Motivation peaks at enrollment and crashes after the novelty wears off. Without a structured re-engagement mechanism, 40-60% of enrolled learners become inactive by week 6. HR then pays for seats that aren't being used.\n\n**Pain 4: HR has no visibility without calling the vendor.** If checking learner progress requires sending an email to a customer success manager and waiting 48 hours, HR managers stop checking. This creates an accountability vacuum that makes renewal conversations awkward.\n\n**Pain 5: One-size-fits-all cohorts.** A 20-person cohort at BlueTech might include A1 beginners and B2 near-fluent employees. Standard group classes fail both ends of the spectrum.\n\n**Pain 6: Scheduling friction.** Group sessions require calendar coordination across a team. If even two employees can't attend, the session feels wasteful. Async + AI-driven practice removes this friction entirely.\n\n**Pain 7: No link to career progression.** Employees don't know what level they need to hit to qualify for a promotion or international project. Training feels pointless without a visible finish line.\n\n---\n\n## 6. How Successful B2B Language Programs Prove ROI for Renewal\n\nThe companies that win renewals do five things:\n\n**A. Pre-launch baseline capture.** They run a diagnostic assessment before any training starts — CEFR level, confidence survey, scenario performance. This creates the \"before\" state. Without it, there's nothing to compare.\n\n**B. 90-day progress narrative.** Monthly summaries sent automatically to the HR manager and line managers — not data dumps, but narrative: \"Team A completed 47 speaking scenarios. Average clarity score improved 18%. 3 employees advanced one CEFR level.\"\n\n**C. Manager testimony collection.** At day 60 and day 90, they send a single-question email to managers: \"Rate your team member's English communication clarity this month vs. last month.\" This produces third-party evidence.\n\n**D. Business metric correlation.** Even weak correlations presented in business language: \"During the training period, cross-functional meeting frequency with the Korean engineering team increased by 2x. Participants report confidence as the driver.\"\n\n**E. Renewal-ready report package.** A formatted PDF — not a spreadsheet — delivered 4 weeks before contract end. It contains: CEFR progression table, engagement heatmap, manager satisfaction scores, scenario completion rate, and a recommended next-phase plan. This document does the renewal selling without a sales call.\n\nCase study benchmark (Komatsu Cummins via Bridge Languages): 97% CSAT, NPS of 65, 600+ employees trained. The renewal argument is in those three numbers, not in the curriculum design.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Certifications That Matter in the Vietnamese Corporate Market\n\n**TOEIC dominates for FDI/manufacturing companies (BlueTech profile):**\n- TOEIC is the standard English proficiency credential for multinational corporations in Vietnam, particularly in manufacturing, electronics, finance, and customer service sectors.\n- Companies like Amkor Technology (semiconductor manufacturing) explicitly require TOEIC ≥ 700 for training and HR roles.\n- TOEIC focuses on listening and reading — the skills most relevant to workplace communication in a non-speaking-intensive role.\n- It is the credential employees can put on a resume to access better positions.\n\n**IELTS for management, global projects, and postgraduate:**\n- Required for employees applying for international assignments or postgraduate study.\n- Carries more prestige than TOEIC at the individual level.\n- More expensive and less directly job-relevant for factory/tech floor employees.\n\n**VSTEP for civil service and university requirements only:**\n- A Vietnamese government-issued certificate used primarily for university admission and public sector employment.\n- Carries almost no weight in FDI companies or private-sector tech/manufacturing firms.\n- Vietnamese universities themselves increasingly prefer IELTS over VSTEP.\n- Do not build any NET assessment framework around VSTEP unless targeting public universities.\n\n**Strategic recommendation for NET:** Align Obi's progress tracking and milestone certificates to TOEIC band language (e.g., \"You are now performing at a TOEIC 600-650 level in business email writing\") rather than IELTS or VSTEP. This creates a visible career credential pathway that HR managers can communicate to employees and CFOs.\n\n---\n\n## 8. The Decision-Making Process at a Company Like BlueTech\n\nThe average B2B buying committee in 2025 has 10-11 stakeholders. For a 50-500 person Vietnamese FDI company, the real chain is:\n\n1. **HR Manager (champion):** Identifies the need, researches vendors, builds the internal case. Usually a woman in her 30s with an HR or education background. She has tried 1-2 previous providers that failed. She is bought by: visible tracking dashboard, \"I don't have to chase vendor for reports,\" and proof the previous provider's problems are solved.\n\n2. **HR Director / L&amp;D Head (approver):** Cares about: does this look professional to leadership, will this survive a CFO challenge, does it integrate with our existing HR software.\n\n3. **CFO (budget gatekeeper):** Activated when contract value exceeds ~$5,000 USD. Cares only about: cost per measurable outcome. The renewal-ready report package (see section 6E) is what gets past the CFO.\n\n4. **Procurement (compliance gate):** Needs a vendor registration, tax invoice capability, and sometimes a formal tendering process for contracts above certain thresholds.\n\n5. **Employee representatives (quiet veto):** If employees complain the platform is boring or useless within the first 30 days, the HR Manager hears about it. Negative word-of-mouth inside the company kills renewal even if the data looks fine.\n\n**Timeline:**\n- Month 1-2: HR Manager researches, gets demos\n- Month 2-3: Internal proposal written, pilot requested\n- Month 3-4: Pilot with 10-20 employees\n- Month 4-5: Pilot results reviewed, budget request submitted\n- Month 5-6: Contract negotiated and signed\n\n**The renewal decision:**\n- Happens 4-6 weeks before contract end\n- Driven almost entirely by the quality of the data HR Manager can put in front of HR Director\n- If employees are still active and the manager impact signal is positive, renewal is near-automatic\n- If engagement dropped after week 8, the vendor gets a competitor demo\n\n---\n\n## 5 Concrete Hardening Ideas for NET's B2B Offering\n\nThese address the specific gap between what NET has now (portals, dashboards, AI coaching) and what makes a Vietnamese corporate client unlosable.\n\n---\n\n### Hardening 1: The TOEIC Compass — Credential-Anchored Milestone System\n\nBuild every Obi session output to include a TOEIC-equivalent band estimate. After each AI coaching session, Obi produces: \"Based on today's session, your business email writing is performing at approximately TOEIC 550-600 level. To reach 700 (the BlueTech promotion threshold), focus on: [3 specific gaps].\"\n\nThis does three things simultaneously: gives the employee a visible finish line tied to their career, gives HR a credential-adjacent metric for the CFO, and differentiates NET from every other platform that uses generic CEFR labels that mean nothing to Vietnamese HR managers.\n\n---\n\n### Hardening 2: The Zero-Effort Weekly Report — Automated HR Manager Inbox\n\nEvery Monday at 8am, NET sends the HR Manager a one-email summary: 5 numbers, 3 sentences, no login required. Format:\n\n&gt; **BlueTech English Training — Week 14**\n&gt; Active learners: 34/47 (72%) | Avg speaking practice: 23 min | Top scenario this week: Email escalation | 2 employees advanced to next TOEIC band | 3 learners inactive &gt;7 days → Obi sent re-engagement nudge automatically\n\nThe HR Manager does not need to log in, does not need to ask anyone, and has something to forward to the HR Director in 10 seconds. This single feature kills the biggest renewal risk (HR feels disconnected from progress) and has zero equivalents among local Vietnamese providers.\n\n---\n\n### Hardening 3: The Manager Impact Loop — Third-Party Evidence Engine\n\nAt days 30, 60, and 90, NET automatically sends a 2-question SMS/email to each enrolled employee's line manager (added at onboarding):\n1. \"Rate your team member's English communication clarity this month: 1-5\"\n2. \"One thing they've improved: [free text]\"\n\nResponses feed directly into the corporate dashboard and the renewal-ready report. This generates third-party testimony that HR can show the CFO without any extra work. The manager receives one SMS every 30 days — low friction. The impact: renewal decisions are no longer based on vendor-provided data but on the manager's own words.\n\n---\n\n### Hardening 4: The Learnship Displacement Play — Emergency Rescue Offer\n\nLearnship collapsed into insolvency in December 2025 and was acquired by goFLUENT. Companies in Vietnam and SEA that were using Learnship are in contract limbo right now (Q1-Q2 2026 window). NET should build a specific \"Learnship Migration\" landing page and outreach sequence:\n\n- Offer a 2-week free pilot migration: import their cohort, run baseline assessments, deliver week-1 report\n- Guarantee weekly automated reporting from day 1 (Learnship's biggest complaint was opaque reporting)\n- Price the first 3 months at a pilot rate to reduce switching risk\n\nThe displacement window is approximately 6-9 months before goFLUENT stabilizes the product and re-signs those clients. This is a one-time market opening.\n\n---\n\n### Hardening 5: The Renewal Package — CFO-Ready PDF, 4 Weeks Before Expiry\n\nNET auto-generates a formatted renewal document 4 weeks before each corporate contract end. It contains:\n- CEFR/TOEIC-band progression table per employee (before vs. after)\n- Engagement heatmap (who's active, who improved, who needs support)\n- Manager impact scores (from the Manager Impact Loop)\n- Top 3 business scenarios completed by the cohort\n- Recommended next-phase plan with specific cohort groupings\n- Cost-per-TOEIC-band-advancement metric (e.g., \"Average cost to move one employee up one TOEIC band: 2,400,000 VND\")\n\nThis document exists in no other local Vietnamese English training provider's toolkit. It removes the renewal sales call — it is the sales call. HR Manager sends it upward; CFO approves. The cost-per-outcome framing is what converts a \"training expense\" into a \"human capital investment\" in Vietnamese corporate language.\n\n---\n\n## Summary Assessment\n\nNET's current position — 50,000 VND/hr, AI coach, student portals, employer dashboards, weekly reports — is structurally correct but under-articulated in corporate procurement language. The platform has the bones; it needs:\n\n1. TOEIC band anchoring on all Obi outputs (credentialing)\n2. Zero-login automated Monday email to HR (visibility)\n3. Manager impact SMS loop (third-party evidence)\n4. Learnship displacement outreach (timing)\n5. Auto-generated renewal PDF package (CFO conversion)\n\nThe biggest structural advantage NET has over Western platforms is price. At $2/hr for AI-augmented coaching vs. $7-15/user/month for self-study apps with no human element, NET's value proposition in Vietnam is asymmetric — provided the reporting layer communicates in corporate language rather than edtech language.\n\n---\n\nSources:\n- [Corporate English Enablement: 6 KPIs L&amp;D Can Track in 90 Days — Talkparty](https://www.talkparty.app/blog/corporate-english-enablement-kpis)\n- [Measuring English Training ROI for L&amp;D Teams — Talkparty](https://www.talkparty.app/blog/measuring-english-training-roi-for-ld-teams)\n- [The EF Corporate Learning Maturity Report 2026](https://corporatelearning.ef.com/en/resources/articles/2026-language-training-maturity-report/)\n- [10 Best Corporate Language Training Platforms for 2026 — Talaera](https://www.talaera.com/business-english-platforms/best-corporate-language-training-platforms/)\n- [Measuring the ROI of Corporate Language Training — Bridge Languages](https://bridge.edu/languages/en/measuring-corporate-language-training-roi/)\n- [5 Corporate Language Training Challenges &amp;
